PART IV. NOTIFICATIONS, During the Quarter ended June 80, 1898.
190 CUSTOMS GAZETTE. [APRIL-JUNEj NOTICES TO MARINERS. CHINA SEA. No. 320. FOOCHOW DISTRICT. SUNKEN ROCK IN COW&#39;S HORN PASS, NEAR THE NORTHERN ENTRANCE TO HAITAN STRAIT. NOTICE is hereby given of the existence, to the westward of Brown Rock, Tessara group of islands, of a rocky patch, about half a cable long in a north and south direction by about three-quarters of a cable wide, and having two pinnacles, about 30 yards apart, near its middle, on which there is a depth of not more than 4 feet at low water of spring tides. To this rocky patch the name &quot; Namoa Rock &quot; has been given. From the two pinnacles of the patch the highest part of Brown Rock bears E. I S., the highest part of Fairway Island S. I E., and the Cow&#39;s Horn S.W. j W. DInTIoNs. Vessels coming through Haitan Straits from the southward, if wishing to use the channel to the westward of the Tessara group, should, after passing Slut Island, steer so as to bring Brown Rock open to the eastward of Fairway Island, N. I E. Keep this mark on until Cow&#39;s Horn bears W.N.W., which will lead clear of the rocks lying 21 miles S. by E. of Cow&#39;s Horn Point, and steer so as to pass about 2 cables to the westward of Fairway Island. Then gradually bring the west point of Shingan Island in line with the west end of Fairway Island, S. I W.J which will lead about 2 cables west of Brown Rock. Keep this mark on, being careful not to open Shingan Island west of Fairway Island when nearing the Namoa Rock, until the southernmost point of Great Dragon Island (the north-easternmost island of the Tessara group) is open to the northward of Brown Rock, when a N.E. by N. course may be steered. Brown Rock may be passed safely at a distance of I cable to the westward of it. The s.s. Namoa struck on the patch of *rocks&#39; described in the foregoing on the 3rd October 1897 and became a total wreck. The local fishermen, though they were offered a liberal reward to point out any other sunken dangers in the passage west of Brown Rock, stated that they did not know of any. SHANGHAI, 28th April 1898.
1898. NOTIFICATIONS. 191 No. 321. TIENTSIN DISTRICT. CHANGE IN POSITION OF LIGHTSHIP &quot;TAKU.&quot; NOTICE is hereby given that in consequence of the Bar having extended further seaward, the lightship Taku has been moved 9j cables E. by S. I S., magnetic, from her old position, and that she is now moored in 19 feet at low water spring tides. Approximate position:- Latitude........... . . . 380 54&#39; 36&quot; N. Longitude........... . . 117* 51&#39; 46&quot; E. SHANGHAI, 20th JUne 1898. No. 322. YANGTZE RJVER-CHINKIANG DISTRICT. BETHUNE POINT BEACON LIGHT. NOTIcE is hereby given that the Bethune Point Beacon Light has been shifted 200 yards N. 84* E. from its last position, in consequence of the washing away of the land on which it stood. SHANGHAI, 20th June 1898. No. 323. YANGTZE RIVER-KIUKIANG DISTRICT, POINT HAINES BEACON LIGHT. NOTICE is hereby given that the Point Haines Beacon Light has been shifted 91 yards S. 460 E. from its last position, in consequence of the washing away of the river bank. SHANGHAI, 20th June 1898.
192 CUSTOMS GAZETTE. [APRIL-JUNE 1898.) No. 324. SWATOW DISTRICT. MUD FLAT BUOY DISCONTINUED. NOTICE is hereby given that the Mud Flat Buoy, No. 2 in the published List for I898, has been removed, and that mooring dolphins have been erected 70 feet outside of the position it occupied. SHANGHAI, 20th Je I898.

APPENDIX I. KOWLOON AND LAPPA: QUARTERLY RETURNS OF TRADE. APRIL-JUNE 1898.
206 CUSTOMS GAZETTE. [APRIL-JUNE, (Received i6tA Judy 1898.) CUSTOM HOUSE, KOWLOON, 9TH JULY 1898. SIR, I-a. In conformity with the instructions contained in your Circular No. 381, Second Series, I now transmit to the Statistical Department at Shanghai, for publication, the statistics of the trade at Kowloon and the neighbouring stations in Native vessels for the second quarter of the year 1898, April-June. b. The Custom House (Au a U) was opened on the 2nd April 1887, and the collection of Provincial Likin and Ching-fei Tax on General Cargo, at the rates fixed by the Provincial Tariff received from the Viceroy of the Two Kwang, began on that day. The collection of Tariff Import Duty and Convention Likin on Opium (Hk.2b 30 and Hk.2&amp;amp; 8o per picul) commenced on the i4th April 1887, and the collection of Native Duties on General Cargo, at the rates fixed by the Native Custom House Tariff received from the Hoppo, on the ist July 1887. The stations at present controlled by the Kowloon Customs are known as the Capsuimoon ( * ), Changohow ( g), Fotochow ), and Kowloon (J g) stations. c. The word &quot;Inwards&quot; in these statistics means to China, and the word &quot;Outwards,&quot; from China. I. 1898.] LAPPA. 217 (Beceived i8th Jqly 1898.) CUSTOM HOUSE, LAPPA, nITH JULY 1898. I.-a. In conformity with the instructions contained in your Circular No. 381, Second Series, I now transmit to the Statistical Department at Shanghai, for publication, the statistics of the trade of this port for the second quarter of the year 1898, April-June. b. The Custom House ( ) was opened on the 2nd April 1887. The collection of Tariff Import Duty and Convention Likin on Opium (Rk.Th 3o and Hk.2 8o per picul) and the collection of ProvincialLikinand Ching-fei Tax on General Cargo, at the rates fixed by the Provincial Tariff received from the Viceroy of the Two Kwang, com- menced on that day. The collection of Native Duties on General Cargo, at the rates fixed by the Native Custom House Tariff received from the Hoppo, commenced on the Ist July 1887. The stations at present controlled by the Lappa Customs are known as the Malowchow (A &#39; 69) and Chienshan (&#39;$ Lii) stations, with three sub-stations, known as the Shekkok (;U M), Kuttai (&quot; ), and Kwanchiap ( j) sub-stations. c. The word &quot; Inwards &quot; in these statistics means to China, and the word &quot; Outwards,&quot; from China. II.
